- **[2026-08-17]**: docs(designs): reconcile `upgrade-project-content-sync-plan.md` with reality, found while checking whether recent `upgrade-project.ts` work was reflected in design docs — the spec registry already correctly listed the design as `"status": "implemented"`, but the document body's own status checkbox still said `"구현 대기 중"` (implementation pending), even though `semverGt`/`extractScriptVersion`/`extractFrontmatterVersion`/`fileHash` and the full `SYNC_IF_NEWER` (scripts/agents/skills) + `docs/_common/` OVERWRITE behavior it specifies are all live in `upgrade-project.ts` today (predates the 2026-07-19 `v1.7.0` follow-on work per CHANGELOG, exact implementation commit unconfirmed) — marked implemented. Documented a real limitation discovered during a same-day `Projects/co-deck` upgrade as a new `"알려진 한계"` (known limitations) section: `extractScriptVersion` only matches `// @version` line-comment headers, so `scripts/helpers/security-validator.ts` (which declares its version inside a JSDoc block comment) is silently treated as "no version" and permanently excluded from sync — this caused a real crash when a newer `audit.ts` (synced) started hard-importing an export that only exists in the newer `security-validator.ts` (never synced, stuck on 1.0.1, because of this exact gap). Bumped `docs/specs/registry.json`'s `last_updated` for this entry to match. Also fixed an unrelated, previously-uncaught `lifecycle-sync-audit.ts` failure: `templates/co-deck/scripts/co-deck/handbook/scaffold-handbook.ts`'s `@version` was bumped to 1.1.0 in an earlier same-session change but `templates/co-deck/scripts/co-deck/SCRIPTS.md`'s registry entry was never updated to match (and the version bump itself had never actually been committed to this repo, only used locally to test `upgrade-project.ts`'s comparison logic) — both fixed together. `bun scripts/lifecycle-sync-audit.ts` — 0 errors (was 1).

- **[2026-08-17]**: fix(co-deck): backport real fixes and a genuine new feature from the diverged `Projects/co-deck` fork into `templates/co-deck/skills/handbook` — comparing all 15 handbook scripts across the two forks (initially misjudged as ~6,000 diff lines; after stripping a systemic missing-`@version`-header line-shift artifact, the real diff was ~420 lines across 9 files) found: (1) a genuine pre-existing bug in `templates/co-deck` — 5 scripts (`scaffold-handbook.ts`, `check-tables.ts`, `deploy-handbook.ts`, `validate-handbook.ts`, `validate-nav.ts`) had `// @version` before the `#!/usr/bin/env bun` shebang, which Bun requires to be the literal first line — verified these scripts were **completely unrunnable** (`bun run scaffold-handbook.ts ...` threw a syntax error) both before and after this session's own edits, confirmed pre-existing via `git show HEAD:...`; (2) 4 stale header-comment paths (`check-search.ts`, `check-structure.ts`, `handbook-doctor.ts`, `update-footers.ts` all said `scripts/X.ts` instead of their real `scripts/co-deck/handbook/X.ts` location); (3) `scaffold-handbook.ts` was missing a real feature `Projects/co-deck` has — `--title`/`--description`/`--repo`/`--chapters` CLI args driving auto-generated `README.md`/`README_ko.md`/`LICENSE` (CC BY-NC-SA 4.0) files, plus wiring `deploy-handbook.ts` into the scaffolded output and its `package.json`'s `deploy` script (previously copied nowhere and referenced by no script). Verified end-to-end: ran the fixed `scaffold-handbook.ts` against a scratch directory and confirmed real README/LICENSE/package.json output, then ran the skill's own `apply-handbook-theme.test.ts`/`scaffold-theme-style.test.ts` (25 pass) and `check-authoring.ts --examples-dir` (0 errors) to confirm no regression. Documented the new scaffold args in `BUILD_GUIDE.md`. Everything else compared — `check-authoring.ts`, `handbook-doctor.ts`, `apply-handbook-theme.ts`, `deploy-handbook.ts`'s command-execution layer — templates/co-deck was already ahead (private-repo-reference enforcement, correct 2-layer dark-mode migration the `Projects/co-deck` fork never received, a same-file-only duplicate-ID check vs. a version in the fork that would false-positive on every legitimate multi-language handbook, and shell-injection-hardened `execFileSync`/argv-array subprocess calls vs. the fork's raw `execSync` string interpolation) — left untouched, and flagged as two separate follow-up tasks to port back into `Projects/co-deck` rather than scope-creeping them into this change. `handbook` skill 0.2.3→0.3.0. Deliberately did not propagate to the `.claude`/`.gemini`/`.agents` platform mirrors (already independently diverged before this session, confirmed by diff — `platform-skill-lifecycle-manager`'s job, not a manual copy). Also spawned a follow-up task for `AUTHORING_GUIDELINES.md` §22 still documenting the old 3-layer dark-mode design after the code migrated to 2-layer, found during the same comparison.

- **[2026-08-16]**: docs(co-deck): backport 5 markup/navigation lessons from a live handbook debugging session into `handbook` skill's `AUTHORING_GUIDELINES.md`/`QUALITY_CHECKLIST.md` (root `skills/` copy only — see Notes) — a user-reported "card wasn't made properly" / "table wasn't made properly" complaint against two external handbooks (`multi-agent-harness-handbook`, `intro-to-ai-harness`, both under `Handbooks/`, both built from this skill) turned into a multi-round debugging session that found real, generalizable authoring bugs, not just content typos. Added: **§11-3** (no `<a>` nested inside `<a class="card">` — invalid HTML5 that the parser silently repairs by truncating the outer link, so half the card stops being clickable with zero visible symptom in a static preview); **§11-4** (`.table-schedule`'s `td:nth-child(n+3) { white-space: nowrap; }` is scoped to short-vocabulary schedule tables and blows out table width when misapplied to a table whose 3rd+ column holds sentence-length prose — found because a skip-map table's "recommendation" column was in that position); **§21-4 point 5** (grep body-text chapter-number cross-references, e.g. Korean `N장`/English `Chapter N`, against the actual current `index.html` chapter list — the existing 4 renumbering-integrity checks only covered link labels, not running prose, which is exactly where a real "Chapter 5" vs "Chapter 6" self-contradiction was found and missed by every automated checker); extended **§21-4 point 3** to explicitly cover standalone new pages (not just renumbering) and *every* language variant of `site-search.js` registration (found: 3 new translated pages were silently unsearchable); and a new CI tip in `QUALITY_CHECKLIST.md` to verify SHA-pinned GitHub Actions actually resolve via `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/git/refs/tags/<tag>` before committing — found because 3 of 4 pinned SHAs in one handbook's `deploy-pages.yml` were subtly wrong, silently failing every deploy for two commits' worth of history with no visible error beyond an easily-missed Action Summary. Added matching rows to both docs' pre-ship/manual-review checklists. `handbook` skill 0.2.2→0.2.3.
  - **[2026-08-16]**: **Notes / deliberately out of scope**: (1) The `.claude`/`.gemini`/`.agents` mirror copies of these two reference docs were checked and found to have *already* independently diverged from the root `skills/` copy in unrelated sections (e.g. §12-1's register-consistency wording) — a blind overwrite would have destroyed that divergence, so this change intentionally touches only the root `skills/handbook/` copy; propagating to the mirrors is `platform-skill-lifecycle-manager`'s job, not a manual copy, and is left as follow-up. (2) The handbook-specific fix that actually needed the `5throck/setup` install-script repo (clone-directly, not vendor-or-submodule) was judged too specific to this one workshop's tooling to generalize into a topic-agnostic skill template, and was intentionally left out per explicit user scoping. (3) The underlying content bugs and both broken CI workflows were already fixed directly in the two external repos this session (`5throck/multi-agent-harness-handbook` PRs #97–#105, `5throck/intro-to-ai-harness` PRs #19–#21); this change only updates the ai_workspace-side authoring guidance so future handbooks built from this skill don't reintroduce the same five bugs.

- **[2026-08-16]**: docs(co-deck): specify a canonical, hardened `copyCode()` implementation in the `handbook` skill's `AUTHORING_GUIDELINES.md` (§2, all 4 platform mirrors: `.claude`, `.gemini`, `.agents`, root `skills/`) — the prior guidance only named the `copyCode(this)` pattern without a concrete implementation, which let two live handbooks built from this skill (`multi-agent-harness-handbook`, `intro-to-ai-harness`, both external repos under `Handbooks/`) independently hand-roll incompatible copy-button behavior: one added a second helper (`copyPromptBox()`) to support multi-line `.prompt-box` targets, the other never did, producing visible copy-button drift between otherwise-sibling handbooks. The new guidance pastes the actual verbatim function (joins sibling `<code>` elements with `\n` when a box has more than one, falls back to the existing single-target `previousElementSibling`/`pre` behavior otherwise) and explicitly forbids inventing separate per-purpose copy helpers going forward. `handbook` skill 0.2.1→0.2.2 (all 4 mirrors). Both external handbook repos were already fixed directly (5throck/multi-agent-harness-handbook#95, 5throck/intro-to-ai-harness#18); this change only updates the ai_workspace-side authoring guidance so future handbooks built from this skill don't reintroduce the same drift.

- **[2026-08-16]**: docs(adr): cross-reference the Governance Backlog design doc from ADR-0055 — user asked whether the Governance Backlog work had been reflected in "the design and related documents"; a re-audit found `docs/adr/0055-spec-registry-enforcement.md` never mentioned it, despite ADR-0055's own Stage 2 being the ticket system's first real tracked entry. Added a link + the actual ticket path (`tickets/governance/T-20260816-001.yaml`) to the Stage 2 section and a `References` entry pointing back to `docs/designs/2026-08-16-governance-backlog-design.md` and `AGENTS.md` §3.7.5. `AGENTS.md`, `CONSTITUTION.md`, and `docs/constitution/09-operations-workflow.md` were already correctly wired (checked, no changes needed there) — the gap was specifically the ADR not linking forward to the mechanism that now implements its own Stage 2 gate. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean.

- **[2026-08-16]**: feat(governance): add a reusable Governance Backlog for deferred, judgment-gated decisions — extended the existing `kind: manual` ticket system (`scripts/ticket.ts`) instead of adding a new registry, per the same "don't build another file nobody checks" reasoning already applied to the spec registry this session. Added an optional `not_before` (ISO `YYYY-MM-DD`) field to `Ticket` — an eligibility gate orthogonal to `status`, reusing `spec-register.ts`'s UTC-normalized date convention for the comparison. `ticket.ts` gains `create --manual ... --not-before <date>` and two independent, composable `list` filters: `--kind <service|manual>` (didn't exist before — `list` only supported `--status`) and `--ready` (status in `backlog`/`waiting` AND `not_before` unset or already past). Surfaced at two existing touchpoints rather than a new one: `CONSTITUTION.md`'s session-start checklist (step 6, silent when nothing is ready) and the Weekly Health Check (`docs/constitution/09-operations-workflow.md` §9.1). The PM Gateway dispatch convention for acting on ready items — normal dispatch path, optionally an Agent Teams teammate for independent work — was deliberately placed in `AGENTS.md` §3.7.5 rather than `agents/pm.md`, a finding from a `/meeting` design review (architect + auditor): `agents/pm.md` has an L0→L1→L2 extends chain into every variant, but `ticket.ts` is `@l2-propagate: false` (workspace-root only) — a variant PM would otherwise inherit a reference to a script it doesn't have. **Found and fixed during implementation, not caught in the design meeting**: `.gitignore` has `tickets/*.yaml` (deliberately — service-ticket instances are ephemeral/local by design, per its own "Task 8" comment), which would have silently defeated the entire point of a Governance Backlog: a ticket created under the original flat `tickets/` directory never leaves the local machine, contradicting "surfaces automatically at the next session, on any machine." Fixed by routing `kind: manual` tickets to a new `tickets/governance/` subdirectory (in `ticket.ts`'s CLI layer only — `ticket-store.ts`'s functions already took an explicit directory parameter, no store-layer change needed) rather than editing `.gitignore` — `tickets/*.yaml`'s single `*` doesn't cross directory boundaries in gitignore syntax, so the subdirectory was already untracked-but-not-ignored (verified with `git check-ignore`). `list`/`board` now merge both directories; `move` resolves whichever directory actually has the ticket file; `next`/`doctor` are unchanged (service-only concepts). Registered the first real entry: Stage 2 of `ADR-0055` (promote the spec-check relevance warning to a blocking failure), `not_before: 2026-08-23` (PR #538 merge date + 7-day soak period), now correctly tracked at `tickets/governance/T-20260816-001.yaml`. Added 5 boundary-case unit tests (unset/past/exactly-today/future/done-ticket-excluded) to `tests/unit/ticket-store.test.ts` — the exactly-today case specifically exercises the `<=` vs `<` boundary. Cron-based notification was discussed and intentionally left as a documented-but-not-executed follow-up (a live scheduled task is a standing configuration change requiring separate confirmation). `ticket.ts` 1.0.0→1.1.0, `ticket-schema.ts`/`ticket-store.ts` 1.0.0→1.1.0. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean; `bun run test:unit`/`bun run test` pass (same pre-existing unrelated flaky test as prior PRs this session). Design doc: `docs/designs/2026-08-16-governance-backlog-design.md`. Meeting transcript: `memory/meeting-2026-08-16-governance-backlog-design.md`.

- **[2026-08-16]**: feat(governance): implement Part B of spec-registry-enforcement — harden `project-to-variant.ts` (161 lines) instead of duplicating the mature 1143-line `l3-to-variant-pipeline.ts`. Added a complexity/divergence routing check (default: >40 variant-unique files, or >3 domain directories with >15 files each not present in `templates/common/`) that now automates the judgment call previously buried only in `SKILL.md` prose — prints a recommendation to use the Full L2 Pipeline and aborts unless `--force` is passed, with thresholds overridable via `--threshold-files`/`--threshold-dirs`. Replaced 2 of the 5 "Manual Review Checklist" items with actual automation: `--design-doc <path>` now auto-runs `spec-register.ts` (closing the same registration gap Part A targeted), and `regenerate-agents-md.ts --variant <target>` now runs unconditionally to regenerate the roster (mechanical, not gated on judgment) — leaving only the genuinely judgment-based items (`pm.md` override review, `CLAUDE.md`/`GEMINI.md` narrative context) printed. Found and fixed a real, previously-silent bug while wiring this up: the script's auto-generated `variant.json` produced plain-string `agents`/`skills` arrays (`["lead"]`) instead of the canonical `{name, file}` object shape every real variant uses, which made the new `regenerate-agents-md.ts` call silently fail with "Agent file not found: agents/undefined.md" — nothing had previously consumed that malformed shape together with `regenerate-agents-md.ts` to expose it. Verified end-to-end with disposable fixture projects under `tests/.temp/`: small fixture doesn't trigger the routing check, large fixture (63 files, 3×20-file domain dirs) correctly aborts without `--force` and proceeds with it, and a real (non-dry-run) conversion correctly auto-registers a spec and regenerates a well-formed `AGENTS.md`. Also fixed `skills/project-to-variant/SKILL.md`'s stale CLI usage section (documented `<project-path> [--name] [--type]`, which never matched the script's actual `--source`/`--target` flags). `project-to-variant.ts` 1.0.3→1.1.0, `project-to-variant` skill 1.0.0→1.1.0. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean. Design doc: `docs/designs/2026-08-16-spec-registry-enforcement-design.md` (Part B, now marked implemented) — Stage 2 (blocking spec-check enforcement) remains the only deferred item, still gated on a soak period.

- **[2026-08-16]**: feat(governance): implement Part A Stage 1 of spec-registry-enforcement (ADR-0055, Proposed) — fixed two bugs that made `--spec-check` a permanent no-op: `audit.ts`'s guard was `SPEC_CHECK && !LIFECYCLE_ONLY`, but `dev-sync.ts`'s only call site passes both flags together, so the block never ran; and even when reached, the relevance check only verified *some* spec was `approved`/`implemented` anywhere in the registry, not whether it was relevant to the current diff. Replaced it with a diff-recency heuristic (diff touches `docs/specs/`/`docs/designs/`, or an active spec was `last_updated` within 7 days) and removed `.quiet()` from the `dev-sync.ts` call site so warnings are now visible — still non-blocking (`Warn`, not `Fail`) for this Stage 1 pass. Added `scripts/spec-backfill.ts` (new, `--dry-run`/`--check` modes) to retroactively register `docs/designs/*.md` files predating `spec-register.ts` adoption; ran it to register all 45 previously-unregistered design docs (registry: 4 → 49 entries), and wired `--check` into the Weekly Health Check cadence (`docs/constitution/09-operations-workflow.md` §9.1) so the gap can't silently reaccumulate. `spec-register.ts` gained a `proposed` `SpecStatus` value (already used by a live registry entry but outside the declared type) and an `--id` override flag (backfilled entries need a computed `YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>` id for files without a date-prefixed filename — 47 of 48 lacked one). `audit.ts` 2.12.0→2.13.0, `dev-sync.ts` 1.5.2→1.5.3, `spec-register.ts` 1.0.1→1.1.0. Stage 2 (promote the relevance warning to a blocking `Fail`, after a soak period) and Part B (`project-to-variant.ts` hardening) remain deferred follow-up PRs per the design doc's Delivery Scope. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean; `bun run test:unit`/`bun run test` pass (one pre-existing, unrelated flaky test — `hook-post-write-lifecycle.test.ts`'s "reads git diff" case borderline-timing-out at bun's 5s default — left untouched, not part of this change). Design doc: `docs/designs/2026-08-16-spec-registry-enforcement-design.md`.

- **[2026-08-16]**: docs(designs): add spec-registry-enforcement design doc — investigated why the June 2026 spec-registry drift-detection mechanism (`workflow-integrated-methodology-design.md`) never worked in practice: `dev-sync.ts` calls `audit.ts --spec-check --lifecycle-only`, but the check's guard (`SPEC_CHECK && !LIFECYCLE_ONLY`) is always false under that exact flag combination, so the block never executes; even if it did, the relevance check (`hasActiveSpec`) only verifies *some* spec is active anywhere, not whether it's relevant to the current change; and the call site uses `.quiet().nothrow()`, silencing any output. `docs/designs/2026-08-16-spec-registry-enforcement-design.md` specifies a 2-stage fix (visible+relevant now, blocking after a soak period), a new `spec-backfill.ts` script to retroactively register the 44 of 48 `docs/designs/*.md` files currently missing from `docs/specs/registry.json`, and a scoped hardening of `project-to-variant.ts` (identified as the actual bottleneck in the "existing project → variant template" pipeline, vs. the already-mature `l3-to-variant-pipeline.ts`). Design only in this PR — no code changed yet; implementation is follow-up work.

- **[2026-08-16]**: fix(security,templates): resolve the 17 WARN-level findings surfaced by the two new `audit.ts` checks added earlier today (shell-injection scan, ADR-0050 script-drift check) — `templates/co-deck/scripts/co-deck/handbook/deploy-handbook.ts`'s 13 shell-injection-shaped `gh`/`git` call sites now use a new argv-array `runArgs()` helper (`execFileSync`) instead of string-interpolated shell commands, which is injection-resistant regardless of input validation; `bun scripts/audit.ts` now reports 0 shell-injection WARNs workspace-wide (was 13). `templates/co-abap/scripts/co-abap/`'s 4 flagged near-duplicates of `templates/common/scripts/` were refactored per ADR-0050 Part 1: `dispatch-parallel.ts` and `dispatch-serial.ts` now import and compose the common versions instead of re-implementing dispatch/pipeline logic (82.0%→59.4% and 89.0%→67.2% line-overlap similarity — the remaining overlap is CLI-parsing/wrapper boilerplate the line-based heuristic can't distinguish from duplication); `dispatch.ts` was left as-is (pure variant-agnostic CLI router that already delegates to the co-abap wrappers, high similarity is expected); `retry-handler.ts` was deliberately kept separate with an added comment documenting genuinely VSP-specific behavior (AbortSignal cancellation support, randomized jitter for parallel-dispatch thundering-herd avoidance, simplified error classification) that doesn't exist in the common version. `deploy-handbook.ts` 1.0.0→1.1.0; co-abap `dispatch-parallel.ts`/`dispatch-serial.ts`/`dispatch.ts` 1.0.0→1.0.1, `retry-handler.ts` 1.0.0→1.0.2. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean (0 Fail); remaining WARNs on the 4 co-abap files are the line-overlap heuristic's known limitation with composition-based refactors, not unaddressed duplication.

- **[2026-08-16]**: feat(audit): close 3 regression-coverage gaps surfaced by the August audit — no general shell-injection static scan existed outside the narrow `shellCommandPatterns` module (despite commit `bd4312f62` having fixed real shell-injection-adjacent bugs), ADR-0050 had sat in "Proposed" status with its own Part 1 (variant script inheritance) follow-up never actually built, and `validate-agents.ts` had no regression tests despite 2 same-day bugs in its frontmatter parsing. Added `checkShellInjectionPatterns()` and `checkVariantScriptDrift()` as new WARN-only checks to `scripts/audit.ts` (+ `templates/common/scripts/audit.ts`), backed by a new `sourceShellInjectionPatterns` export in `scripts/helpers/security-validator.ts` (+ L1 mirror); added `tests/unit/validate-agents.test.ts` with 2 regression tests, exporting `parseFrontmatter`/`hasNestedField`/`isAgentFile` and parameterizing `validateRuntimeDefinitions()` from `scripts/validate-agents.ts` (+ `templates/common/scripts/validate-agents.ts`) for testability, with no behavior change; and flipped ADR-0050 from Proposed to Accepted with a new Implementation section. The new checks found real, pre-existing drift, both flagged (WARN) rather than fixed in this pass: 13 shell-injection pattern warnings in `deploy-handbook.ts`, and 4 variant-script-drift warnings in `templates/co-abap/scripts/co-abap/` (`dispatch-parallel.ts` 82.0% similar to its `templates/common` counterpart, `dispatch-serial.ts` 89.0%, `dispatch.ts` 90.6%, `retry-handler.ts` 89.0%) — remediating the drift is separate follow-up work. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean (0 Fail, only the new WARNs). The regression tests were verified to actually catch regressions: both original `validate-agents.ts` bugs were manually re-introduced, confirmed to fail the new tests, then reverted. Design doc: `docs/designs/2026-08-16-august-regression-coverage-design.md`.

- **[2026-08-16]**: fix(skills): fix `skill-lifecycle-audit.ts`'s scope/owner validation for root-level (L0) variant-scoped skills, and fix `presenter-mode`'s genuinely orphaned owner — the audit's `isValidScope()` only accepted `workspace`/`common`/`variant`/the current working directory's basename, which can never match a `co-*` variant name when the audit runs from workspace root (where these L0 skills actually live); its `agentExists()` likewise only checked root `agents/` and `.claude/agents/`, missing agents that live solely in a variant's own `templates/co-*/agents/` roster. Both now also check known `templates/co-*` variant names/agent rosters. This resolved 7 of 8 pre-existing warnings without touching the skills themselves (`accessibility-audit`'s `scope: co-design`, `mece-logic-auditor`'s `scope: co-consult` + `owner: strategy-analyst`, `sarif-exporter`'s `scope: co-security`, `stride-threat-matrix`'s `scope: co-security`, `sound-synth`'s `owner: sound-designer` were all already correct — just previously unrecognized as valid). The 8th, `presenter-mode`'s `owner: presentation-architect`, was a real orphan (no such agent exists anywhere in the repo) — reassigned to `html-build` (the `co-deck` agent responsible for HTML/JS build concerns, matching `presenter-mode`'s BroadcastChannel-based dual-window sync). `skill-lifecycle-audit.ts` 1.2.1→1.3.0, `presenter-mode` 1.0.0→1.0.1, both synced across L0/L1/L2 platform mirrors (`.claude`, `.gemini`, `.agents`) via `sync-skills.ts`/`sync-skills-to-l2.ts`/`propagate-to-templates.ts`. `bun scripts/skill-lifecycle-audit.ts` now reports 0 warnings (was 8); `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean.

- **[2026-08-16]**: fix(hooks): fix `pre-push.ts` gitleaks scan range for first-time branch pushes — the "new branch, no remote counterpart" fallback built its `git rev-list` exclusion range by joining every `refs/remotes/*` SHA into a single string (`"sha1 sha2..localOid"`), which is not valid git revision syntax once more than one remote ref exists — always true in practice, since `origin/HEAD` sits alongside `origin/main`. This broke `git push` for any brand-new local branch (surfaced while pushing the error-handling migration batch: `git rev-list` failed, the broad `catch` swallowed the real error, and the hook reported it as a false "Secret scan failed" instead of the actual git syntax error). Fixed by emitting each remote SHA as its own `^sha` exclusion token and passing the token array directly to Bun's `$` (which expands arrays into separate argv entries) instead of pre-joining into one interpolated string. `pre-push.ts` 1.2.7→1.2.8, synced to `templates/common/scripts/hooks/pre-push.ts`.

- **[2026-08-16]**: fix(scripts): migrate second batch of scripts to the `scripts/lib/error-handling.ts` standard (ADR-0054) — `validate-md-language.ts`, `verify-memory.ts`, and `validate-model-registry.ts` now use `die()` for their fatal/exit paths (uncaught top-level errors, CLI usage errors, missing/unreadable `workspace-schema.json`, missing `models` block or platforms, unreadable `agents/` directory) instead of raw `console.error`+`process.exit(1)`. Each fatal branch is guarded by `if (import.meta.main)` so importing the module (e.g. from a test) still logs the error without killing the test process — the same pattern already used by the first migration batch's `agent-list.ts`/`validate-doc-folder.ts`. Structured violation/mismatch report output was left untouched (plain `console.log`/`console.error`) per ADR-0054 §"What NOT to Do" — it's a validation report, not an error/exit path. All 3 scripts are L0+L1; synced to `templates/common/scripts/`. `validate-md-language.ts` 1.5.0→1.6.0, `verify-memory.ts` 1.0.1→1.1.0, `validate-model-registry.ts` 1.0.3→1.1.0.

Also re-ran `bun scripts/verify-readme-sync.ts --update-hashes` and patched `translated_from_hash` in the affected `README_ko.md` files so EN/KO hash-pairing stays consistent. `co-design`, `co-abap`, `co-news` needed only this hash housekeeping (their README content was already accurate). Root `README.md` and `templates/common/README.md`'s pre-existing `PLACEHOLDER` hash issue is out of scope (task scope is `templates/<variant>`, not workspace-root/L1-common) and was left untouched. `bun scripts/verify-readme-sync.ts` now shows `[PASS]` for all 10 `templates/co-*` variants; `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean.

Left `AGENTS.md § 3`'s detailed content in place (added a cross-reference note instead of deleting, to avoid risk to this governance-critical file) and added a pointer from `docs/co-abap.context.md`'s Development Workflow section. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean. Added the missing row + explanatory note to bring the SSOT in line.

- **[2026-08-15]**: fix(gitignore): add `.zcode/` (Antigravity/ZCode session-local state — plans, config) to `templates/common/.gitignore`'s `WORKSPACE-MANAGED` block and explicitly to the L0 root `.gitignore` — discovered that `.zcode/plans/*.md` session-plan files and `.zcode/config.json` were tracked in git in 5 of 6 `Projects/co-*` repos (co-abap, co-architect, co-consult, co-game, co-news), since the L1 template never had this exclusion and each project's own `.gitignore` predates it. Only `co-deck` had already added it manually. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean.

- **[2026-08-15]**: fix(security): propagate the `.env$` gitleaks path exclusion from L0 to L1, add dependency-directory exclusions — discovered while unblocking `/sync` for `Projects/co-consult` and `co-architect`: both hit a pre-push secret-scan block from `gitleaks detect --no-git` (whole-filesystem scan mode, ignores `.gitignore`) finding real API keys in their local `.env` files. `.env` is correctly gitignored and was never part of either commit — L0's own `.gitleaks.toml` already excludes `\.env$` (with a comment explaining exactly this), but that exclusion was never propagated to `templates/common/.gitleaks.toml`, so every L2/L3 project scaffolded from it inherited the gap. Also added `.venv/`, `venv/`, `node_modules/`, `__pycache__/` path exclusions (both L0 and L1) after co-architect's scan picked up 15 more false positives from third-party Python library internals inside a 283MB gitignored `.venv/`. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean.

- **[2026-08-15]**: changed(policy): allow the `lang: ko` + `lang_reason` exception in `agents/*.md` and `skills/*.md` (user-directed) — previously these paths were hard-blocked from the exception mechanism entirely, which made it impossible for a project whose real-world domain genuinely requires Korean (e.g. a Korean architecture firm's `bylaw-expert` agent citing Korean building codes, bilingual client-facing skill docs) to pass `validate-md-language.ts` without either fabricating an English-only version of legally-sensitive source material or forking the tooling. Core governance/routing docs (`CLAUDE.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `CONSTITUTION.md`, `AGENTS.md`, any `*.context.md`) remain hard-protected — this only relaxes agent/skill *content* files, and still requires an explicit `lang: ko` + valid `lang_reason` (`legal`/`source-material`/`proper-noun`) declaration, not blanket permission. Updated the matching documentation in `CLAUDE.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `CONSTITUTION.md` (+ `templates/common/` L1 mirrors) and softened `CONSTITUTION.md`'s "Non-English Reference Material in Skills" section, which had assumed the exception was categorically unavailable in skills. `scripts/validate-md-language.ts` v1.4.4→1.5.0, propagated L0→L1. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean.

- **[2026-08-15]**: fix(scripts): `skill-lifecycle-audit.ts`'s frontmatter regex didn't normalize CRLF line endings before matching, so any SKILL.md with Windows line endings (e.g. several skills freshly synced into `Projects/co-news` by `upgrade-project.ts`) was reported as having "No valid frontmatter found" even though it was well-formed — `---\r\n` doesn't match the literal `---\n` the regex expected. Normalized `\r\n`→`\n` before matching, same fix pattern already applied to `validate-agents.ts`. v1.2.0→1.2.1, propagated L0→L1.

- **[2026-08-15]**: fix(scripts): `validate-agents.ts`'s frontmatter regex anchored `---` to the literal start of the file (`^---\n`, no `m` flag), so any agent file with a leading comment before its frontmatter (e.g. co-deck's extends-pattern `pm.md`, which starts with `# @resolved-from: L0/common/agents/pm.md`) was treated as having no frontmatter at all — even though it had a complete, valid `lifecycle:` block. Added the `m` flag so `^---` matches the frontmatter delimiter on any line, not just line 1. v1.0.3→1.0.4, propagated L0→L1. Discovered while fixing missing `lifecycle` frontmatter across several `Projects/co-*` repos.

- **[2026-08-15]**: fix(scripts): `validate-agents.ts` excluded `README.md` from agent lifecycle-frontmatter validation but not `README_ko.md`, causing false-positive "missing lifecycle frontmatter" errors on Korean README files in `agents/` — discovered while unblocking `/sync` for several `Projects/co-*` repos. Broadened the exclusion regex to `^README(_\w+)?\.md$` plus any underscore-prefixed file (`_COMMON.md`, etc.), matching the convention used elsewhere. v1.0.2→1.0.3, propagated L0→L1. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean.

- **[2026-08-15]**: fix(templates): bump `version:` on all 13 `templates/co-game/agents/*.md` files — discovered while syncing `Projects/co-game` with the model-registry update: the same content-changed-without-version-bump bug affected co-game's non-extends agent roster, whose `tier:` comment lines were updated in that earlier PR without bumping `version:`, so `upgrade-project.ts` silently skipped them too. All 12 files 1.0.0→1.0.1, `pm.md` 1.1.0→1.1.1. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean.

- **[2026-08-15]**: fix(templates): bump `version:` on the 5 tier-corrected agent files from the prior commit — discovered while checking whether the tier fix would actually reach already-scaffolded `Projects/co-*` projects: `upgrade-project.ts`'s `SYNC_IF_NEWER` mechanism compares each agent file's `version:` field between the L2 template and the L3 project, and silently skips the sync if versions match. The tier-only edit hadn't bumped these, so the fix would never have propagated. `templates/co-consult/agents/data-analyst.md` 1.0.0→1.0.1, `templates/co-deck/agents/html-build.md` 1.2.1→1.2.2, `image-curator.md` 1.3.0→1.3.1, `pdf-export.md` 2.1.0→2.1.1, `measure.md` 2.0.0→2.0.1. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean (Agent audit/validation green).

- **[2026-08-15]**: fix(templates): correct 5 miscalibrated agent tiers found in a workspace-wide tier review against AGENTS.md §3.6's High=complex-reasoning / Medium=review-QA / Low=mechanical-execution philosophy — `templates/co-deck/agents/{html-build,image-curator,pdf-export,measure}.md`: Medium→Low (each is a mechanical execution step against an already-locked spec — templating, fetch-by-query, fixed export pipeline, spec-merge validation — comparable to `version` (already Low), not to judgment-based agents like `source-verifier`); `templates/co-consult/agents/data-analyst.md`: Low→Medium (role description covers "advanced ... statistical modeling," which is analytical work, not rote execution). Updated all 4 `tier:` platform lines per file plus every AGENTS.md tier-table/detail-section mention in both variants (co-deck: roster table, deliverables table, dispatch-mode table, and 4 per-agent detail blocks; co-consult: roster table, deliverables table, dispatch-mode table, and 1 per-agent detail block) — deliberately left `diagram-specialist` at Medium (a Python-regex first pass briefly mis-touched it via a substring match on "image-curator" in its own row's parallel-execution note; caught and reverted before commit). `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean.

Unlike ADRs/dated decision records (left unchanged), these are active specs a reader would treat as live guidance. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean.

- **[2026-08-15]**: chore(models): update the model registry (user-directed) — `docs/workspace-schema.json` `models.claude`: high `claude-opus-4-7`→`claude-opus-5-0`, medium `claude-sonnet-4-6`→`claude-sonnet-5-0`, low unchanged (`claude-haiku-4-5`); `models.gemini`/`models.antigravity`/`models.gemini-cli`: medium/low `gemini-3.5-flash`→`gemini-3.7-flash`, high unchanged (`gemini-3.1-pro`). Also updated the 8 L0 `agents/*.md` tier comments, `CONSTITUTION.md`, `docs/constitution/05-multi-agent-architecture.md`, and the `explain-me` skill's `PLATFORM_HARNESS.md` (root + `.claude`/`.gemini`/`.agents` copies). - **[2026-08-15]**: feat(hooks): add `scripts/hooks/agent-model-gate.ts` — a `PreToolUse` hook (Claude Code CLI, matcher: `Agent`) enforcing the 3-Tier Cost Optimization strategy (CLAUDE.md §6 / AGENTS.md §3.6). Root cause: every `agents/*.md` frontmatter has `model: inherit` (correct default), so any `Agent()` dispatch that forgets to pass `model` explicitly silently runs the subagent on the parent session's model instead of its documented tier — High (`opus`) and Low (`haiku`) dispatches are the ones that visibly break, since Medium (`sonnet`) often matches the parent by coincidence (user-reported: the 3-tier strategy wasn't working, specifically for opus and haiku). The hook asks for confirmation whenever `subagent_type` matches one of the 8 workspace-root tiered agents (`pm`, `architect`, `auditor`, `lifecycle-manager`, `automation-engineer`, `docs-writer`, `scaffolding-expert`, `security-expert`) and `model` is missing or not a valid alias, naming the correct tier alias to add. Registered in `.claude/settings.json` `PreToolUse`. L0-only (`scripts/SCRIPTS.md` Layer=L0) — not propagated to `templates/common/`, since variant projects have their own agent rosters without this workspace-wide tier registry. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean; hook manually verified against 4 stdin payload cases (missing model → ask; explicit model → pass; non-tiered subagent_type → pass; non-Agent tool → pass).

- **[2026-08-15]**: fix(scripts): make `propagate-to-templates.ts`'s CONSTITUTION.md scrub comment-aware — discovered while validating the L0-L3 code alignment: `scrubConstitutionRefs()` blindly replaced every literal `CONSTITUTION.md` occurrence with `context.md` when copying a script to `templates/`, including inside functional file-existence checks like `existsSync(join(dir, "CONSTITUTION.md"))`. This silently broke L0-root detection in already-propagated L1 copies of `agent-lifecycle-audit.ts`, `readme-lifecycle-audit.ts`, `skill-lifecycle-audit.ts`, `audit.ts`, `test-platform-parity.ts`, and others (12 files total) — each was checking for a `context.md` marker that never exists at any workspace root. Rewrote the scrub function to be comment-aware for code files (`.ts`/`.js`/`.tsx`/`.jsx`): only text inside `//` and block comments is scrubbed; string literals in functional code are left untouched. Markdown/prose files keep the existing full-replace behavior (no functional code to protect there). Re-ran `propagate-to-templates.ts --apply`, which correctly detected and re-copied all 12 previously-broken L1 files. `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean (including a zero-width-space character I accidentally introduced while drafting the fix, caught and fixed by the audit's character-hygiene check).

- **[2026-08-09]**: feat(governance): codify & enforce that variants MUST NOT carry a local `docs/context.md`. Principle: the immutable project context is owned solely by `templates/common/docs/context.md` (L1) and copied verbatim into every project; variant-specific content belongs in `docs/<variant>.context.md`. Codified across `CONSTITUTION.md §10` (also corrected the legacy `_common/context.md` path → `templates/common/docs/context.md`), `docs/constitution/07-new-project.md` (SSOT section), `docs/constitution/00-ssot-architecture.md` (SSOT table + violation pattern), and `docs/governance/variant-contract.md` (forbidden-overlay subsection). Enforcement: (1) new `validate-templates.ts` Check **WS-07** `checkNoVariantLocalContextMd` fails any `templates/co-*/docs/context.md` (negative-tested: exit 1); (2) fixed the real root cause of the earlier co-export leak — `generate-variant.ts`'s `SKIP_IN_COPY` already listed `docs/context.md` but the remaining-files guard compared raw `file.targetPath` (backslashes on Windows), so the exclusion silently never matched; normalized to forward slashes (mirroring the agent loop), verified end-to-end by promoting the `Projects/co-export` fixture (which still held a stale `context.md`) to a temp output — no `docs/context.md` leaked; (3) `new-project.ts` variant-overlay now skips `docs/context.md` as defense-in-depth so a stray template copy can never clobber common's canonical file. Also resolved a pre-existing L1 duplication: deleted stale `templates/common/docs/_common/context.md` + `_common/phase-definitions.md`, which the `docs/_common/`→`docs/` flatten was using to clobber the actively-maintained top-level versions in every scaffolded project (`_common/` keeps its unique README/security.md). Verified `bun scripts/validate-templates.ts` (0 errors, WS-07 passes for all 8 variants), `bun scripts/audit.ts` (all checks passed), `bun scripts/verify-scripts.ts` (0 warnings), pipeline 6/6 phases.

- **[2026-07-22]**: fix(workspace): resolve findings from a second full `/project-review` pass, several of which were regressions/gaps in the prior pass's own fixes — (1) `scripts/dev-sync.ts` computed the memlog date via `toISOString()` (UTC) instead of local time, so a `/sync` run near local midnight could misfile (or duplicate) the session summary into the wrong day's `memory/YYYY-MM-DD.md`; confirmed empirically (`memory/2026-07-21.md` and `memory/2026-07-22.md` both carried the same session-summary block for one commit) and switched to a local-date computation; (2) the `?tocStyle=...` URL-parameter path in `docs/html-themes/themes/_shared/ppt-engine.js`'s `initPPT()` set `data-toc-style` directly from the query string with no validation, fully bypassing the `ALLOWED_TOC_STYLES` allowlist just added to the `postMessage` handler in the prior pass — applied the same allowlist check to the URL-param path; (3) `themes/pitch/template.html`'s own `initAll()` duplicated the URL-param/`postMessage` tocStyle handling *without* any origin or allowlist validation (a second, unguarded copy alongside the shared, now-hardened `initPPT()` it also calls) — removed the duplicate; also fixed pitch's remaining `base.css`/`theme.css` `<link>` paths, which the prior pass's CSS-path fix had left inconsistent with sibling themes (only `ppt-engine.css` was corrected then); (4) found and fixed a more serious, previously-undetected bug in `scripts/co-deck/lib/theme-builder.ts`: the `<html>`-attribute-injection regex (`/<html\s+([^>]*)>/`, no anchor) matched the *first* literal occurrence of `<html ...>` text in the file — which is inside a `template.html` doc-comment describing the injection in prose (e.g. `` Injects <html data-theme="..." data-style="<style>"> ``) — so the real `<html>` tag downstream was never touched by the non-global `replace()`, silently shipping decks with the *source template's hardcoded placeholder* `data-style` (wrong for every style except the one hardcoded in that theme's source) and no `data-toc-style` at all; anchored the regex to line-start (confirmed via `outline_academic.html`, which had rendered with `data-style="classic"` instead of `academic`); (5) `scripts/generate-scripts-readme.ts`'s generated banner falsely claimed the file was "generated from `templates/common/scripts/SCRIPTS.md`" when it only ever reads the workspace-root `scripts/SCRIPTS.md` — corrected the banner text; (6) `GEMINI.md`'s PostToolUse-limitation line told Gemini/Antigravity users to manually run `bun scripts/audit.ts` as the hook replacement, omitting `post-write-lifecycle-check.ts` entirely (the same gap fixed in `CLAUDE.md` during the prior pass but missed here) — added the correct script name and clarified the two are distinct stages. Regenerated all 27 co-deck preview decks to embed the corrected runtime and `<html>` attributes. Verified via `bun test scripts/co-deck/tests/` (190/191 pass, same pre-existing environment-limited failure as before) and `bun scripts/audit.ts` (all checks passed)
- **[2026-07-22]**: fix(templates/co-deck): port genuine fixes discovered in `Projects/co-deck` (an L2 project instance) back into the L1 template — (1) `docs/html-themes/themes/_shared/ppt-engine.js` `_updateVoiceDropdown()` had a duplicated/corrupted code block (a botched partial copy referencing out-of-scope `voice`/`option`/`nameSpan` variables, with a stray unmatched `});`) that produced invalid generated JS in every theme's inlined runtime; removed the duplicate. Also hardened the `postMessage` TOC-style listener to validate `event.source === window.parent` and the payload against an allowlist (`glass-drawer`/`solid-drawer`) instead of trusting any message unconditionally, and ported the matching explanatory comment to `preview/preview.html`; (2) `scripts/co-deck/build-theme-deck.ts` was missing the `join` import from `path` despite calling `join()` three times — a guaranteed runtime crash on any invocation; fixed the import and wired `presentation.tocStyle` from `lecture-profile.md` through to `buildThemeDeck()`; (3) `scripts/co-deck/lib/theme-builder.ts` gained the corresponding `tocStyle` option (sets `data-toc-style` attribute, defaults to `glass-drawer`) and a new validation pass that parses every generated inline script block as a runtime function body to catch JS syntax errors (like the ppt-engine.js corruption above) before shipping a broken deck; this incidentally fixed a pre-existing failing test (`theme-builder.test.ts` — `should set data-toc-style on <html> element`). Regenerated all 27 preview decks via `build-theme-preview.ts --all` to embed the corrected runtime. Verified via `bun test scripts/co-deck/tests/` (190/191 pass — the one remaining failure is a pre-existing, environment-limited browser-subprocess test unrelated to these fixes) and `bun scripts/audit.ts` (all checks passed)

Found and fixed two classes of violation across all 7 variants: (1) `.agents/skills/` was badly under-populated everywhere (e.g. co-consult 2/15, co-deck 0/10, co-security 0/1) because `sync-skills.ts` only ever ran against the workspace root — added `--dir <path>`/`--all-variants` flags (v1.4.0) so it can target any project root, then ran it across every variant; (2) 11 domain skills (`service-design`, `ui-ux-design-intelligence` in co-design; `code-review`, `refactoring`, `test-driven-development` in co-develop and co-game; `api-documentation`, `documentation-writing`, `research-analysis` in co-work) existed ONLY as `.claude/skills/`/`.gemini/skills/` copies with no `skills/` SSOT entry at all — migrated each into its variant's `skills/` (adding required frontmatter fields and required section headers to pass `audit.ts`'s Variant skill sections check), registered in `SKILLS.md` and `variant.json skills[]`, then redistributed via `sync-skills.ts --all-variants`. Also fixed co-game's `skills/SKILLS.md`, which was a corrupted auto-generated file pointing at absolute paths inside `Projects/co-game` (a generated project instance, not the template) instead of the template itself. Follow-up fix: `sync-skills.ts` v1.4.1 excludes `security-gate: true` skills from Phase 1 mirroring (validate-templates.ts Check B-03 requires those stay platform-neutral, `skills/`-only) — the v1.4.0 `--all-variants` run had blindly overwritten co-security's stale-but-compliant `.claude`/`.gemini`/`.agents` `verify-authorization` mirrors with the current SSOT content (which now carries `security-gate: true`), breaking that gate; those 3 mirror directories were removed

- **[2026-07-19]**: fix(skills): resolve skill-lifecycle audit gaps found while auditing all skills across the workspace and 7 variants — (1) `scripts/skill-lifecycle-audit.ts` `scope` check only accepted the literal string `variant`, flagging 26 legitimately-scoped skills (`scope: co-consult`, `scope: co-deck`, `scope: co-security`) as invalid even though `scripts/helpers/layer-filter.ts` treats any non-`workspace`/non-`common` value as a valid variant-name marker (ADR-0032 §7); fixed the audit to accept `workspace | common | variant | <current project's own directory name>`, and excluded `docs/_examples/skills/**` (illustrative documentation, not real skills) from scanning; (2) `security-scan`/`sync`/`validate-docs-links` (`scope: common`) were owned by `security-expert`/`lifecycle-manager`/`docs-writer` respectively — these specialist agents are intentionally not propagated to `templates/common/agents/` (ADR-0043) nor defined by any of the 7 variants, so the 3 skills audited clean at the workspace root but were orphaned in every variant; reassigned all 3 to `owner: pm` (root + `templates/common` + `.claude`/`.gemini`/`.agents` mirrors via `propagate:apply`), updated both `SKILLS.md` registries; documented both fixes in `docs/constitution/06-skill-lifecycle.md` (§6.2 scope semantics, multi-location audit procedure, "common-scope skill owner pitfall" guidance) so future skills don't regress the same way
- **[2026-07-19]**: feat(co-consult): document the "Non-English Reference Material in Skills" pattern — `skills/*.md` can never carry the `lang: ko` exception (CONSTITUTION.md Language Policy), so a skill needing source-language reference data (glossaries, official field/status name mappings) must store it in a non-Markdown file under `skills/<name>/references/` instead, since `validate-md-language.ts` only scans `.md`; added to `CONSTITUTION.md`, `docs/constitution/06-skill-lifecycle.md` (§6.7), and both `templates/common/docs/context.md` variants; applied to the k-dart skill as the first example — added `references/terms-ko.json` (Korean-original DART terminology: report types, corporate actions, financial line items, audit opinions, entity classes, status messages) across all 4 platform copies, and fixed a pre-existing undeclared Korean status string (meaning "normal"/"OK") that had leaked into 3 of the 4 copies

- **[2026-07-18]**: feat(handbook): port selective improvements from the multi-agent-harness handbook to the co-deck handbook skill — NOT a blind overwrite (the co-deck `skills/handbook/` is a divergent fork, ahead on several files). JS assets: `inpage-search.js` gains a TreeWalker `acceptNode` filter (rejects SCRIPT/STYLE/highlight-class/SVG-namespace nodes — prevents inline-SVG corruption) plus a `?q=` URL-param bridge from `site-search.js` result links; `lang-switcher.js` gains an `IS_FILE_PROTOCOL` guard (fixes every option disabling under `file://`) and trailing-slash index handling (fixes 404 on landing pages). - **[2026-07-11]**: docs(governance): add Sequential Branch Dependency Rule (§3.3) to workspace root and project templates — codifies a lesson from this session's PR merge process: 6 PR branches were all created from the same stale `main` commit without merging any first, so every one conflicted on shared pipeline files (CHANGELOG.md, memory logs, VERSION_MANIFEST.md, generated READMEs) that `dev-sync.ts` touches on every commit regardless of workstream; sequential *merge* order could not resolve this since the conflicts were baked in at branch-*creation* time; new rule requires merging a prior open PR before branching for the next task unless parallel branching is explicitly justified in the plan's Trade-offs section; added to `docs/constitution/03-pr-workflow.md` (summary in `CONSTITUTION.md` §3, cross-reference in `AGENTS.md` §5.1) **and propagated to `templates/common/CLAUDE.md`/`GEMINI.md`** so projects scaffolded via `new-project.ts` inherit the discipline from day one
- **[2026-07-11]**: test(security): recreate `tests/unit/ssrf-safe-fetch.test.ts` (M15 regression suite) — discovered missing from `main` despite the corresponding `scripts/lib/ssrf.ts` `safeFetch()`/`fetchPinned()`/`SSRFBlockedError` changes having merged via PR #399; the test file was never actually included in that commit; 5 tests covering blocked-range rejection, redirect refusal, and the core TOCTOU-closing property (pinned connection succeeds against a non-resolvable hostname, proving no fresh DNS lookup occurs)
- **[2026-07-11]**: fix(security): M15 — close the real SSRF TOCTOU DNS rebinding gap, not just document it — `validateUrl()` alone never closed the window: both call sites (`ingest-security-frameworks.ts`, `ingest-external-skills.ts`) validated DNS once, then called the global `fetch(url)` separately, which performs its own independent DNS lookup (verified empirically that Bun's `fetch()` silently ignores a custom `dns` init option); added `safeFetch()`/`fetchPinned()` to `scripts/lib/ssrf.ts` using `http(s).request()`'s `lookup` override (verified empirically that this option IS honored) to connect directly to the pre-validated address — exactly one DNS resolution per request, never re-resolved; rejects 3xx redirects (matching prior `redirect: 'error'` behavior) rather than following them; both ingest scripts updated to use `safeFetch()`; 5 regression tests including one that proves no fresh DNS lookup occurs by connecting via a non-resolvable test hostname pinned to a local server
- **[2026-07-11]**: fix(docs): M11/M12 — fix AGENTS.md heading §-prefix inconsistency across SSOT and all 7 variants — root `AGENTS.md` had `§3.1`-`§3.8` prefixed but `4.1`-`5.3` unprefixed; the "co-design AGENTS.md format cleanup" deferred item was actually this same root SSOT inconsistency, inherited by every variant via propagation, not a co-design-local issue; fixed root + propagated to templates/common via `propagate:governance`/`propagate:docs`, then directly fixed the 7 variant copies (the `§4`/`§5` region is not covered by the `COMMON-AGENTS` marker-inject block, so it required a direct per-variant fix); verified anchor links (e.g. `AGENTS.md#51-standard-execution-plan-template` added in the prior governance-docs-consolidation change) are unaffected since GitHub's slug generation strips `§` identically whether present or not; also re-fixed the `MAP_PATH is not defined` crash in `propagate-to-templates.ts` `publishDocs()` found again on `main` (same bug as the pending fix in an unmerged PR, reapplied here since this session's work needed it)
- **[2026-07-11]**: docs(governance): consolidate Execution Plan boilerplate and 3-tier strategy duplication, fix rule conflict — per `docs/reports/governance-docs-diet-analysis.md`, the Execution Plan table/rules (previously duplicated 5x across CLAUDE.md/GEMINI.md/CONSTITUTION.md/AGENTS.md) and the 3-tier model strategy concept (6x, including two non-identical versions within AGENTS.md itself) are now single-sourced in `AGENTS.md` §5.1 and §3.6 respectively, with CLAUDE.md/GEMINI.md/CONSTITUTION.md linking out and retaining only their genuinely platform-specific content (model-ID mappings, thinking_level notes); fixes an active rule conflict where CONSTITUTION.md said to "always include Lifecycle Update and Final QA Audit as the final two steps" while AGENTS.md said the opposite ("no separate rows needed — /sync handles both") — only the correct, current AGENTS.md rule remains; also fixes a pre-existing `MAP_PATH is not defined` crash in `propagate-to-templates.ts` `publishDocs()` (block-scoped const referenced from an outer-scope function) discovered while propagating this change, which was silently blocking the `--docs` L1→L2 governance sync step entirely; propagated to templates/common and all 7 L2 variants

- **[2026-07-03]**: fix(project-review): comprehensive improvement plan from 7-agent parallel project review — Batch 1 (Critical): tsconfig.json creation, unused deps removal, import.meta.main guards (54 scripts), variant contract contradictions resolved, 5 skill lifecycle records created, VERSION_MANIFEST parsing fixed, 2 ghost lifecycle records removed; Batch 2 (High): L0→L1 propagation sync (6 scripts), pre-rebase bypass removed, CVE overrides documented, AGENTS.md heading + Language Policy exception + frontmatter standardization, docs/index.md created, shebang standardized (16 files), empty catches replaced with error logging (8 scripts), import extensions .js→.ts (25 files), 7 template lifecycle records created — 141 files changed

### Added
- **[2026-07-08]**: fix(propagate): close a real footgun in `--include-disabled`, tighten the `docs`-domain note, and log the `propagation-map.json` duplicate as tech debt — code review of the prior disabled-domain fix caught that `--include-disabled --apply` would have silently *written* a domain's files for real (the same `collectDiffs()` result feeds both the dry-run report and the `--apply` write step), contradicting its documented "inspection only" intent; `propagate-to-templates.ts` (v2.1.1) now rejects that flag combination outright (exit 1) instead of relying on prose to prevent it. The `docs` domain's `note` field was a wall of incident narrative; split into a short `note` (reason + activation condition, machine-skimmable) and a separate `history` field for the investigation detail — both fields added to `scripts/lib/propagation-map-schema.ts` (v1.2.0) and `propagate-to-templates.ts`'s `Domain` type so they're not "unknown properties" to tooling. Logged `templates/common/scripts/propagation-map.json` (a required-to-mirror-but-nothing-reads-it duplicate, kept in sync only because `validate-templates.ts`'s glob-based L0/L1 parity fallback matches it by filename) as `docs/templates/known-issues.json` ISSUE-008 rather than fixing it inline.
- **[2026-07-08]**: fix(propagate): implement real glob matching in `propagate-to-templates.ts` and explicitly disable the never-working `docs` L0→L1 domain pending a policy decision — `globFiles()` (v2.1.0) previously only handled two hand-rolled shapes (`*.ext` via `endsWith`, `*/exactname` via `basename` equality) and silently matched **zero** files for any pattern with a wildcard in the filename component itself; the `docs` domain's `include_pattern: "**/*.md"` reduced to `basename() === "*.md"`, which can never equal a real filename, so this rule had propagated nothing since it was declared (verified: `templates/common/docs/` today is a 28-file independently-curated tree — context.md, adr/, variants/ — not a mirror of root `docs/`'s 147 files). Replaced with a proper glob-to-regex converter (`globToRegExp`, unit-tested against 12 cases including the previously-broken pattern) that correctly matches `**/*.md` at any depth including top-level, while producing identical results to the old logic for every other domain's `*.ext`/`*/SKILL.md`-style pattern (verified via before/after dry-run: still 91/91 in sync, zero regressions). Fixing the glob alone would have silently activated ~143 file copies on the next `--apply`, some of them L0-only content (`docs/constitution/`, etc.) that `scripts/audit.ts`'s L0-leakage check explicitly forbids under `templates/` — so `propagation-map.json` (v1.3.0) now marks the `docs` domain `"disabled": true` with a `note` explaining the incident and the open policy question (full mirror vs. whitelist vs. stay independently managed), and `propagate-to-templates.ts` skips disabled domains by default with a visible console notice (previously this kind of gap could go unnoticed indefinitely) plus a read-only `--include-disabled` inspection escape hatch; `scripts/lib/propagation-map-schema.ts` (v1.1.0) documents the new field. No other domain in the map uses a wildcard-in-filename pattern, so this was the only rule affected.
- **[2026-07-08]**: feat(governance): add `scripts/setup-github-branch-protection.ts` (v1.0.1) and reconcile `docs/governance/branch-strategy.md` with actual practice — the script idempotently applies the GitHub repo/branch settings manually configured on this repo today (`delete_branch_on_merge`, `allow_auto_merge`, and required-status-checks branch protection so `gh pr merge --auto` genuinely waits for CI), taking repeatable `--check "job name"` flags rather than a comma-joined list — verified by hand that comma-joining breaks on CI matrix job names like `test (ubuntu-latest, 18.x)`, which already contain commas and get silently mangled by a naive `split(',')`, producing invalid contexts GitHub's API rejects with HTTP 422; `branch-strategy.md` no longer claims a required reviewer approval (impractical for this single-maintainer + automated-`/sync` workflow) or per-branch-type merge methods (`dev-sync.ts` and practice both use merge-commit for every branch type) — both were aspirational text that never matched reality even before today
